the rest day 6, 5 or 0, a day and never a religion; the Hours law `applies`, `trust` for s.30(א)(5) or `unsupervised` for s.30(א)(6); a minor's 18th birthday `youth_until`, null for an adult; `compulsory_education`) and `public.employment_periods` (from, until or open, the last payment once it ended, `joined` for a start copied from the membership and `set` for a holder's, never overlapping: a gist exclusion on the closed day range); both records as hours and wages are (`restrict` keys to the membership, a trigger refuses every delete but a purge that names the business, nobody but the owner writes or truncates them), read like wages (the person while a member, pay holders, a view as an accountant, with the time clock on); `private.backfill_employment_periods()` gave each staff membership one `joined` period from the business's day it began (ended on its last change when inactive) and is safe to run again; `private.track_employment` on memberships (after insert or update of `active`, `role`) opens a `joined` period on the business's day a staff membership starts or comes back, closes the open `joined` one when it stops (inactive, or an accountant now), goes on with the same one when someone is back the day it closed, leaves a holder's `set` period alone, and never fails a membership write over a period; `public.set_payroll_details(actor, business, person, patch, at_time)` for the secret key (only the keys to change, anything else 22023; by the wage's rules: a pay holder, never their own, an owner's only by an owner, never an accountant's; a birth date only from an active owner or the accountant, 42501, a real day in the last 100 years, read once and kept only as `youth_until`, 29 February + 18 years being 28 February; a rest day or Hours law outside the lists is the table's 23514, a number taken 23505; a call that changes nothing writes nothing, and `payroll.details` names the fields that changed, a birth date as `youth_until`, never a value); `public.set_employment_period(actor, business, person, from_day, to_day, paid_until, period_id, at_time)` (adds one, or changes one of the person's, P0002 for another's; by the same rules; an overlap 23P01 'Periods overlap', the end on or after the start, a last payment only once it ended, a start in the last 100 years and at most a year ahead, each said apart; the period is `set` from then on, a `set` one saved as it was writes nothing, and `payroll.period` names the fields that changed, whether it was added and whether a `joined` one was confirmed, never a day); `public.set_hourly_wage` (P4's) refuses a wage before the first period's start once a holder set it (22023 'Before they started'), and its `wage.set` row keeps the minimum of the person's age band on the wage's first day (`private.minimum_wage_for(business, person, on_day)`, under 16, 16 and 17 from `youth_until` as `ageBandOn` reads them, else the adult hour), so a minor's legal wage isn't logged, or read in the activity log, as under the minimum; `public.team_youth(business)` gives owners and managers (and a view as one) who on the staff is a minor today and until when, with the time clock on as for every read of the details, and nobody else anything; the purge (P4's) deletes details and periods before the log; `private.seed_demo_pay` (P4's) ends with `private.seed_demo_details`, from a definition's `details`: payroll numbers from `numbers` in the definition's order for managers and workers, the reset's open periods from `since` (1 March 2026), still `joined`, and the cases `youth` (a minor until the first of the month six months on: 17 through the seeded month), `no_number`, `rest_friday` and `started` (the 10th of three months ago, set by the owner), with no log rows; both seeds name theirs (the shop's Liya a minor with no number yet, Roni resting on Friday, Itai started in the summer; the restaurant numbers only), the hosted definitions are patched by id and both hosted demo businesses reset. pgTAP: `payroll_details.test.sql` new (88: who sets what, the birth date never kept and the log with no number or day, 29 February, an adult, a cleared number, a number taken, periods and their overlap, the trigger on joining from an invite, leaving, coming back after a gap and on the same day, becoming the accountant and a holder's period left alone, a wage before a holder's start, the minimum by age and a minor's wage logged with the minimum of 17, readers, `team_youth` (none with the time clock off), nothing deleting them but the purge, the demo's, the backfill with the trigger off); `demo_reset` (a demo membership deleted after its details and periods under the setting), `hours_kept` (the person who only acted is the business's accountant, who has no employment period to keep their account), `wages` and `lists` (a period purged before an account delete those tests mean to allow), `rls_enabled`. `database.types.ts` gains both tables, `set_payroll_details`, `set_employment_period` and `team_youth`, by hand in the generator's format. Domain: `@domain/youth` (`addYears` as Postgres adds years, `youthUntilOf`, `isMinorOn`, `ageBandOn`, `minorUnderAge`, `youthLabel`), `@domain/typed-date` (three typed numbers to a real day, digits from any keyboard), `@domain/payroll-details` (`REST_DAYS`, `HOURS_LAW`, `detailsOf` with the defaults, `parseDetailsForm`, `detailsFailureOf`, `periodsOf`, `spansOverlap`, `overlapsAny`, `currentPeriod`, `wageFloorOf`, `parsePeriodForm`), with tests; `@domain/wages` gains `wageWarningFor` (the band on the day the wage starts and on each later birthday and change of the table while it stands), `beforeStart` in `parseWageForm` and `wageFailureOf`; `payroll.details` and `payroll.period` in `@domain/activity`. `@db/payroll-details` reads both under the person's session and `team_youth`, and writes both with the secret key. New in @ui: `DateField` (day, month and year typed in three numeric fields, left to right as a date is written, digits only, moving on when one is full, real labels in a fieldset), on `/design`. Screens: `/clock/pay/[person]` has a "פרטים לשכר" card (the number or "חסר" in the warning colour, the rest day, the Hours law, "נוער עד 4.2027" and school for a minor, and each period, today's first, "עובד/ת מ-1.3.2026" by the form of address, with "לפי ההצטרפות לאפליקציה, כדאי לבדוק" for a `joined` one and the last payment when there is one); for those who may, "עריכת הפרטים" opens a sheet (the number, the rest day with "רק אם … ביקש/ה" by the form of address, the Hours law with the section and, under management or personal trust, that courts give it only to the most senior posts and the law then doesn't apply at all, rest-day pay included; for an owner or the accountant "תאריך לידה, אם עדיין לא מלאו 18" in a `DateField` with "לא נשמר. נשמר רק עד מתי זה נוער." and what the date makes of the age before saving; school for a minor; "שמירת הפרטים"), a period row or "הוספת תקופת עבודה" opens one with its start, end and last payment (`DateField`s, the joined note, "שמירת התקופה", an overlap said in words before the database is asked); one's own, an owner's for a non-owner and a view say why in words with no buttons; phone the card under the wage card, tablet in the start column, desktop under the wage in the summary row; the wage card warns by the person's age and each later birthday, starts the day field from a later start, and refuses a day before a holder's start in the sheet; `/clock/pay` marks a minor with "נוער" (the row's one badge, and in the desktop table's name cell), the desktop table has "מספר עובד", and the accent card and the accountant's Home say "חסר מספר עובד ל-…" for staff (not owners) with shifts and no number; `/clock` shows the person's own details (number, rest day, a minor's "until" and a start a holder set) and nobody else's; `/team/[person]` shows a manager "נוער עד" from `team_youth` and nothing else of the details; the activity log reads `payroll.details` ("עדכון מספר העובד וגיל הנוער של ליה כץ", the fields as a list) and `payroll.period` (added, changed by field, or confirmed as copied from joining), leading to `/clock/pay`. `/design/shell`: the pay demo's details (Noa's start copied from joining, Yoav resting on Friday and back after an earlier period, Maya a minor with no number, the accent card's count), the clock's own details, `team/maya` with her "until", two payroll rows in the activity demo, the accountant Home's count; `design.spec.ts` visits `team/maya` and enters a birth date and an overlapping period in the demo. e2e: `payroll.spec.ts` (at every size, the minor's badge, Itai's set start and number, Tamar's start to check, Roni's Friday; on desktop, Galit enters Liya's number and birth date, only the 18th birthday kept, the log by field with no value, the count gone, the owner reading the log, put back after; the owner sets Roni's start, the period becomes the owner's, the log with no day, a wage from before it refused in the sheet, put back after), `view-as.spec.ts` (as accountant the details read and can't be changed); `e2e/db.ts` deletes a membership's details and periods first under the setting, and reads and puts back details and periods. Deviations from the plan: the backfill is a function the migration runs once, so pgTAP can run it again with the trigger off; a `joined` start limits no wage (a business joining today still sets last month's wages: only a holder's start does), and the trigger reopens a period closed the same day instead of opening a second one, and never fails a membership write; `set_employment_period` takes `period_id` (not `period`, a keyword in newer Postgres) and an `at_time`, and refuses a start more than a year ahead; details and periods follow the wage's rules for owners (only an owner sets an owner's) and are written only with the time clock on; a birth date of null clears the youth (from the same circle); `team_youth` is new, so managers without pay access see a minor's "until" (on `/team/[person]`) and nothing else, as the plan's table asks; the worker's own view is on `/clock`, and leaves out a start copied from joining; `parsePeriodForm` and `parseDetailsForm` read `DateField`'s three numbers, and the wage's date stays a date input as P4 built it; no notification for details (the plan has none); activity lines are noun phrases, as P3's and P4's are; CLAUDE.md's "Built so far" doesn't list `DateField` yet (not changed in this run), nor docs/AGENT.md's payroll line, still left for Orel's session. Fixed from review: `team_youth` now asks for the time clock, as the details' policy does; the wage log's minimum follows the person's age (above); `set_employment_period` says a start more than 100 years back in its own words ('A period starts in the last 100 years'); `database.types.ts` writes the details' key to the membership as `isOneToOne: true`, since its columns are the table's primary key; on phone and tablet the month's list says "חסר מספר עובד" beside each person the count counts (`PayPerson.noNumber`, the same rule as the count), the accent card links to `?show=noNumber`, which lists only them, and the accountant's Home row leads there too, with the design twin and a `design.spec.ts` visit; the payroll number's hint and error say digits, Latin letters and dashes, and `parseDetailsForm` reads the number through `cleanTyped`, so a maqaf, an en dash or Arabic-Indic digits are read as "-" and 0-9; `DateField` keeps Arabic-Indic, Persian and full-width digits (`cleanTyped` before the digits are kept); the details sheet reads the birth date apart from the rest (`parseBirth`, now also used by `parseDetailsForm`), so a wrong number neither hides what the date makes of the age nor moves the school switch; the period sheet says that a start copied from joining can be confirmed as it is, and its button reads "אישור תאריך ההתחלה" while the form still matches the stored period, "שמירת התקופה" once anything changes; the period sheet moves the focus to the first wrong field, for the server's errors too; the accountant's e2e types a birth date worked out from the shop's month (17 years before the 15th of the month after next), not 15.4.2009, which would have turned adult on 15 April 2027. Checked: Biome, `next typegen`, tsc and the unit and tooling tests (the Windows-only Biome probe aside); by reading, every changed SQL file's dollar quotes, each pgTAP plan against its assertions, every call's arguments and every column read. Not run here (no database, no CI): pgTAP, `supabase db lint`, `pnpm db:types`, `pnpm test:db`, the build, the e2e and the screens at 390, 820 and 1440 px. Next: P6, the legal hours engine.
- Payroll P6, the legal hours engine (issue #76), on chat/payroll-engine over chat/payroll-details (P5, #100), following the payroll plan's §9 and §13 with its critique applied, Orel's answers of 9 November (rest minutes past 42 a week at 175%/200%, each flagged to check) and his order of 10 November (the estimate behind a team-level `payEstimate`, off by default and on in the demo; no new screens), and P4 and P5 as built. `20261110000700_pay_rules.sql`: `public.set_module_settings` (sealed's, replaced) keeps the punch code for an owner or the team with its own words, adds the pay rules (`workWeek`, `shortDay`, `restStart`, `restStartTime`, `workersSeeEstimate`: 42501 'Only an owner or the team changes the pay rules') and the estimate for the team alone (`payEstimate`: 42501 'Only the team switches the pay estimate'), each checked for its type (22023 'Not a pay rule setting'); `private.seed_demo_pay` (P5's) first lays a demo definition's `settings` (by module key) over its template's, so both demo businesses show the estimate; the hosted definitions are patched by id and their settings set at once, with no reset. pgTAP: `pay_rules.test.sql` new (31). `database.types.ts` unchanged (no signature changed). Domain: `@domain/payroll` (`payrollMonth`, `personPayroll`, `payrollRange`, `bucketAgorot`, `hoursText`, `withoutAmounts`, `ownEstimateShown`, `payrollPersonOf`, `payrollPeopleOf`, `payrollReadRange`): workdays by the local date of the clock-in, paid time to the millisecond with unpaid breaks off and open entries counting nothing, each day's norm (six: 480, 420 on the day before the rest day and the eve of a holiday the person doesn't work; five: 516, 456 on the short day, 420 on those eves; 420 for a night shift of 120 paid minutes between 22:00 and 06:00 counted on instants), the day walked in time order to regular, 125%, 150% and in a rest window 150%, 175%, 200%, then the week's regular and rest-150% time toward 2,520 minutes with a paid holiday or election day not worked counted at its average, the rest's past it flagged `restWeekOvertime`; an `unset` week worked out as five, as six and by each day's smaller norm, the highest Σ minutes × percent kept (ties to six) and noted `workWeekUnset`; s.30 people all regular with `hoursLawRestDay`; a shift after a holiday within 36 hours of the end of the person's last work before it, holiday work as a whole (`holidayNight`); minutes by largest remainder so a day's rates add up to the clock's minutes; wage segments by `wageOn`, amounts rounded per rate and segment, the estimate null while a worked day has no wage; the flags of §9.5 and the month's notes. `@domain/payroll-calendar` (hebcal, server only): the nine paid holidays by Hebrew date and Independence Day from hebcal, each day's window (candle lighting on the eve to havdalah, Rosh Hashana's second day from the first's end, Independence Day 20:00 to 20:00), the eves, the weekly rest by rest day (Saturday from candle lighting or a fixed Friday time for 36 hours and at least until havdalah; Friday and Sunday rests 18:00 to 06:00), election day 2026 07:00–22:00. `@domain/holiday-pay` (`holidayPayLines`, `payrollHistory`, `wouldHaveWorked`, `addMonthsToDay`, `historyMonths`). `@domain/payroll-flags` (the flag and note types, `PAYROLL_FLAGS` by group, the statute's `LIMITS`, the adults' and minors' checks). `@domain/payroll-readings`: every §14 reading an accountant or lawyer must confirm is one field of `PAYROLL_READINGS`, so a correction is one change. `@domain/money` gains `minutesAgorot`, and `wageOn` in `@domain/wages` reads any row with `from` and `setAt`. Every §13 case is a vitest case in `payroll.test.ts` (C1–C30 with C4f, C4u, C7n, C8f, C10s, C16r, C17b, C17c (a), (b) and with no work in the week before, C17d, C19s, C16r, C25 and C28 in both readings, C26b, C28, C29, all passing), with `payroll-calendar.test.ts`, `holiday-pay.test.ts` and `payroll-flags.test.ts`. The time clock's manifest has the six settings with `.meta()` (`payEstimate` at team level, default off; the rest at owner level), their words under `modules.time_clock.settings`, and the engine's words for P7 (`modules.time_clock.payroll.flags`, `.notes`, `.holidays`, `.oneOffRestDays`), checked by `tooling/payroll-words.test.ts`; `tooling/demo-pay-estimate.test.ts` keeps the estimate on in both seeds' demo definitions and in no template. Loaders for P7: `currentPayrollMonth(month)` in `app/(app)/clock/pay/pay-month.ts` (everyone's month for pay holders, every amount dropped while `payEstimate` is off) and `currentOwnPay(month)` in `app/(app)/clock/own-pay.ts` (the person's own month under their own session, null unless `payEstimate`, `workersSeeEstimate` and a wage). `e2e/clock-code.spec.ts` takes the first owner-only mark, since the pay rules carry it too. Deviations from the plan: a fifth domain file for the readings, and `PayrollInput.readings` so C25's other reading is a test (screens never pass it); `shortDay` is a choice of Sunday to Thursday, not the weekday editor, which offers all seven; the pgTAP is its own file; a holiday counts as worked when a paid minute of the person's falls in its window, except that a shift that clocked in before the holiday began counts only by its minutes on the holiday's own date (`eveShiftFromHolidayDate`, §14 accountant 4 and lawyer 2), so the eve's shift past candle lighting keeps the 7-hour eve and the holiday-pay line (C17b), while a shift wholly in the holiday's evening (Yom Kippur's, or Rosh Hashana's second day from Saturday's havdalah) or a night shift from the eve into the day works it: no pay line, no 7-hour eve, and `holidayWorked` with those minutes; the eves leave out a day that is itself a holiday; a paid day not worked counts toward the 42 from its local midnight; `wasScheduled` is added only when the history doesn't show the weekday (C29 over §9.4's fixed list), with `noHistory` and no amount when there is no history; the history's `weeks` are the weeks the person worked, worked out in the domain from three months of entries (no `getMonthTotals`); holiday and election-day lines need an employment period covering the day; ties in `unset` go to six, then five; the weekly rest is the longest stretch without work that starts in the week, to the next work, and a Sunday rester's week runs Saturday 18:00 to Saturday 18:00; `belowMinimumWage` runs over the month's days with a wage, by age on each; adults' hour limits skip s.30 people and a minor's days, and a minor's 9-hour day needs the business's week set to five; `nightWeekOver58` counts the week's hours when it has a night shift; the loader is `currentPayrollMonth`, since P3's `currentPayMonth` already reads the hours list, and it works out the month for the whole staff, owners included (an owner without a wage gets `noWage` and no estimate: P7 decides how an owner shows, as P4 did for the list); `currentOwnPay` is null while `payEstimate` is off too. After review: a shift that starts in a holiday and runs past its end within the 36 hours is holiday work as a whole too (`holidayNight`), so coming in before havdalah never pays less than after; `holidayWorked` carries `paidAt` (`regular` for s.30 people, whose words then say the hours were counted as regular and that holiday pay may still be owed, with no 150% or 250%); the week someone turns 18 is checked by age on each day (the minor's 40 hours over the days before the birthday, the adults' limits over the days from it); every §14 reading now drives the engine in one place: `hoursLawRestRegular` (the other reading pays s.30 rest minutes 150%), `paidAbsenceCountsAt` (`average` or `norm`), the new `holidaysForOtherRestDays` (read by `holidaysFor` in `@domain/holiday-pay` for the engine and the pay lines) and `eveShiftFromHolidayDate`, each with a test of its other value; `getTimeEntries` reads page by page (`allPages` in `packages/db/pages.ts`, ordered by `clock_in` then `id`), since PostgREST stops at 1000 rows without an error and payroll reads a whole business over four months, with unit tests past 1000 rows; `dateIn` keeps one date formatter per time zone. Checked: Biome, `next typegen`, tsc and the unit and tooling tests (the Windows-only Biome probe aside); by reading, the migration's and the pgTAP's dollar quotes, `plan(31)` against its assertions, every call's arguments and the columns read, and that the punch code's messages `own_hours.test.sql` asserts are unchanged. Not run here (no database, no CI): pgTAP, `supabase db lint`, `pnpm db:types`, `pnpm test:db`, the build, the e2e and the screens. Next: P7, the rates and the estimate on screen.
- Payroll P7, the rates and the estimate on screen (issue #76), on chat/payroll-estimate over chat/payroll-engine (P6, #101), following the payroll plan's §10 with its critique applied, Orel's answers of 9 November (every amount "הערכה לפני מס"; workers see their own month's estimate; rest minutes past 42 "לבדיקה"; managers without pay access see no amounts) and his order of 10 November (amounts only while the team's `payEstimate` is on, so a real business reads hours and rates without a shekel until an accountant confirms), and P3 to P6 as built. No SQL: no migration, `database.types.ts` unchanged. Domain: `@domain/pay-rates` (`personRates`: a person's month by rate, by wage with each stretch's workdays, nights and breaks, and by day with its split, kind and remarks; `remarksOf`, what a pay holder checks, minors first then by day, without the flags the screens already say in their own words (open, corrected, waiting, no wage, no number) and with the two s.30 flags apart as how someone is paid (`aboutPayOf`); `toCheckOf`, the remarks and every line of pay for a day not worked; `remarkLines` for the month's card, one line per person and kind with how many more, minors first; `flagValues`, the values of each flag's words from the screen's formatters; `keyedFlags`; `monthEstimate`, the known estimates added and those left out for want of a wage counted; `weeksWorkedOut`; `restNameOf`/`restNameOfAll` naming the rest rates by rest day; `ratesShown`, `daySplit`, `dayTags`; `ownMonthOf`, the worker's own month with their five adult limits and only whether a minor's rule was crossed), with tests; `tooling/payroll-words.test.ts` now holds every flag's words to the values `flagValues` passes, and checks the words of every rate, holiday-pay check and worker's limit. Loaders: `payAccess` moved to `clock/pay/pay-access.ts` (the month's loaders and the engine's read it without importing each other); `pay-month.ts` shares `payrollInputOf` and `payrollReadOf` and adds `currentPayrollPerson` (one person's reads, with the month's notes); `currentPayMonth`, `currentPayPerson` and `currentPayHome` (the accountant's Home, from the same `currentPayrollMonth`) add the engine's month, `payEstimate` and, while it is on and the month has hours, the estimate; `currentOwnPay` returns the clock's `OwnPay` (with the rest day, the business and the person's form of address) and is read by `/clock` and `/clock/history`'s pages. Screens: `/clock/pay` says under its title "הערכה לפני מס, לפי השעות. בלי ניכויים, חופשה, מחלה ונסיעות." or, with amounts off, that the estimate isn't on for the business yet and the hours by rate are here; the hero adds the month's estimate (and who it leaves out for want of a wage) and "N הערות לבדיקה"; the accent card "לפני השכר" adds the engine's remarks in P6's words, each with whose it is and "ועוד N פעמים", at most five, then how many more, and follows `?show=`; phone rows add "1,234.50 ₪ הערכה" and "N הערות לבדיקה"; the tablet's person column shows the month by rate and each shift with its day's split; the desktop table has every rate (the rest columns named by the rest day everyone shares, else for any rest day), night shifts, the wage, the estimate while amounts are on, and the phone row's counts, each its own column ("תוקנו", "משמרות לבדיקה" for those waiting or open, "הערות לבדיקה" for the engine's), and its sheet the month by rate. `/clock/pay/[person]`: "לפי תעריף" (each rate with minutes and, with amounts, what it comes to, a heading row per wage after a raise, then the pay for a holiday or election day not worked with its average and what to check by the person's form of address, then "הערכה לפני מס" or why there is none) with "איך זה מחושב", a sheet with the method in plain words (and for a pay holder that some rules wait for an accountant and how each week of an unset work week was read); "הערות לבדיקה" in words, with the unset work week's note and, for an owner, the way to the clock's settings, the stale minimum wage and the paid absences the app doesn't know; each shift on its day's last line "מתוכן 0.60 נוספות 125%", the kind of day (a holiday or election day by name, Shabbat or the rest day, an eve, a night shift) and its remarks in the warning colour; the desktop days table adds every rate, the kind of day and what to check; night shifts in the hero's line and the summary. `/clock`: the person's own month in shekels, a flat card under their wage ("השכר שלך ב…", the estimate with "הערכה לפני מס", the hours at each rate, holiday and election-day pay with "אם עבדת יום לפני ויום אחרי" / "אם היית אמור/ה לעבוד בו" by their form of address, their adult limits in neutral words, for a minor "כדאי לדבר עם {business}", the wage it used, what it leaves out and that the payslip decides, and the method sheet without the accountant's part); `/clock/history` the same card for the month shown; nothing while `payEstimate` or `workersSeeEstimate` is off or without a wage. The accountant's Home adds the estimate under the hero and "N הערות לבדיקה בשעות" to what to check. Messages under `app.pay.rates`, `rateColumns`, `estimate`, `rateCard`, `holidayPay`, `remarks`, `notes`, `tags`, `method`, `day`, `columns`, `dayColumns`, `app.clock.ownPay` and `app.home.accountant.remarks`, he and en. `/design/shell`: the pay demo runs the real engine over its October (with July to September for holiday pay; Tel Aviv, the work week unset): Yoav's election day paid with what to check, Shmini Atzeret on his day, his night inside his Friday rest, Noa and Shiran working on election day, Maya's short breaks as a minor; `?estimate=off` on `clock/pay`, `clock/pay/[person]`, `today/accountant`, `clock` and `clock/history`; the clock demo gains a Friday past candle lighting and Noa's own November. `design.spec.ts` visits both states and opens the method sheets; `payroll.spec.ts` adds the accountant's month by rate with Liya's evening past 20:00 in words, the desktop's rate columns, Tamar's 175% and Itai's 150%, the method sheet; the restaurant's Yoav's night at 125% and no estimate without a wage; Tamar's own estimate on her clock and history and nothing of hers on Itai's, none with `workersSeeEstimate` or `payEstimate` off while pay holders keep the rates (`e2e/db.ts` `shopClockSettings`, `setShopClockSettings`). Deviations from the plan: the worker's card is flat on `/clock` as the plan has it, but because the punch circle is that screen's hero surface (`hero-surface`), not a month hero, which `/clock` doesn't have (on the history the month is the hero); the desktop table keeps one row per person, so every column is that person's month, and a raise splits by wage on the sheet and the page; the table drops P3's shifts and waiting columns for the rates (the summary keeps them, and "משמרות לבדיקה" counts the waiting with the open); the card shows only with a wage (P6's `currentOwnPay`), so "השכר לשעה עוד לא נקבע" becomes the line for a month partly without one; the worker sees five adult limits and one line for minors, nothing else; an unset work week is one note with the way to the settings, each week's way in the method sheet; the month's card names remarks by person and kind in P6's neutral words, prefixed with the name, instead of sentences like "ליה עבדה אחרי 20:00"; holiday-pay lines count as things to check, and are listed where they are counted (one line per person on the month's card, each line with what to check on it in a person's "הערות לבדיקה"); a month with no hours has no estimate; the desktop days table has a kind-of-day column; no new @ui component, so CLAUDE.md is unchanged (`DateField` is still not in its list); `e2e/screens.spec.ts` is unchanged, since it sweeps /design pages: the pay screens' axe and overflow checks are in `payroll.spec.ts` at every size and their twins in `design.spec.ts`; no activity line or notification, as the plan has none. After review: the worker's card is flat on `/clock`, the punch circle staying the screen's one hero, and the method button lost its on-hero look; a month has an estimate with minutes at a rate or a line of pay for a day not worked (`hasEstimate` in `@domain/pay-rates`), so a month with only holiday or election-day pay shows its lines and estimate on the worker's card as on the pay holder's page, a listed person with neither shows no "0.00 ₪" (the phone row, the table's cell, the estimate row), and the history leaves the card out of a month with nothing to estimate and no shift open, its empty state being the only one; a shift still open is said apart, since the month's hours count it up to now and the engine once it ends: "משמרת אחת עוד פתוחה, ותיספר בתעריפים כשתסתיים" before payroll, on Home and under a person's rates (`PersonRates.open`), and "המשמרת שעוד פתוחה תיספר כאן כשתסתיים." on the worker's card (`OwnMonth.open`); the hero's line says shifts and remarks each on its own and "אין מה לבדוק" only with neither (`payCaption`, `app.pay.captionParts`, on `/clock/pay` and the accountant's Home); `remarkLines` gives a person's pay for days not worked one line, so the month card's lines and "ועוד N" add up to its count, and a person's "הערות לבדיקה" lists each line with its checks; a person's night shifts are in the hero's line, which every shape shows; a `DataTable` that only reads is a region named by its caption that takes the keyboard (axe `scrollable-region-focusable`, for the person's wide days table at 1440), with a wide read-only table on /design; the unused `payAmountsShown`, `rateCard.span` and `rateCard.nights` are gone; P6's per-week `modules.time_clock.payroll.notes.workWeekUnset` and `roundingIgnored` stay, with their test, for P8's file, while the screens say the unset week once (`app.pay.notes.workWeekUnset`, each week a row in the method sheet) and the rounding in `beforePayroll.rounding`, so the two pairs must be changed together; the clock demo's month hours are added from its shifts and the running one, `?state=working` shows the card's open-shift line, the pay demo has Shiran's shift left open on the 29th (24 hours in the hours, none in the rates), and `/design/shell/today/accountant?state=checked` is a month with every shift approved and closed and remarks to check, all four checked in `design.spec.ts`; the restaurant's Yoav e2e reads the month's 125% and a day's split as patterns, since each Thursday is also the five-day week's short day (24 minutes at 125%). Domain: `@domain/payroll-export` (pure): `exportSheets(month, people, meta, words)` gives the four sheets of §11.3 in the business's words, right to left: "סיכום חודשי" a row per person per stretch at one wage that holds a day worked, a day not worked and paid, or a shift (a raise is two rows; a raise after the last day worked, with election day after it, is a row of its own at the new wage, and a month with only a day not worked still shows the wage in force; each shift, day not worked and thing to check on the row of the wage on its day; the stretches are `monthWageSpans` in `@domain/payroll`, which the engine's `byWage` now reads too, from the wages the loader passes with each person), with the wage as set, the stretch, workdays, paid hours, the six rates, night shifts, unpaid break hours, shifts corrected, open (not counted) and waiting, the Hours law, the holidays and election day not worked with their date and average (never an amount) and how many things to check; "פירוט יומי" a row per shift, with its times, those before a correction, paid and unpaid break minutes (each break to the minute, as the engine counts them), net hours (exact, shown to two places, so a column adds up to the summary, which rounds summed minutes once), and on the day's first counted shift only its rates and what kind of day it was (night, eve, holiday or rest, election day: the day's, so a morning shift isn't marked as a later shift's night or Shabbat), how it was recorded (`off` "שעון, העסק לא מבקש קוד" apart from a punch), whether it is open or waiting, who corrected it, when and why; "לבדיקה" a row per thing to check in P6's words, with its rule and section (`app.pay.file.rules`) and "השעות לא שונו" (the s.30 flags, the remarks minors first, no wage, no payroll number, each day not worked with what to check by the person's form of address, and each week of an unset work week in P6's per-week words); "על הקובץ" the business, the month, when and by whom (the team's view said so), the engine version, the minimum wage table's date, the settings, the month's notes and six lines (minutes exact, the split a proposal, the daily hours kept unrounded, a day's rates and marks on its first shift, open shifts not counted, days not worked without an amount, no estimate, no amount and no ID numbers). read by pay holders and a view as an accountant, no grant beyond select, kept by P5's `refuse_payroll_record_delete`); `private.closes_payroll` (an active owner or accountant) and `private.month_closed(business, on_day)`; `public.close_payroll_month(actor, business, for_month, closed_snapshot, with_engine, at_time)` (the secret key; 42501 for a manager, a worker or the team; 22023 for a month not ended by the business's clock, no snapshot or no engine; 23505 closed already; a reopened month closes again, its reopening cleared; logs `payroll.close {month, engine}`) and `public.reopen_payroll_month(actor, business, for_month, why, at_time)` (22023 without a reason, P0002 for a month not closed; logs `payroll.reopen {month, reason}`); `set_hourly_wage` (P5's, copied) refuses a wage from a day of a closed month, 55000 "The month is closed"; `add_time_entry` and `correct_time_entry` (P1's, copied) still take a shift of a closed month, and their log rows gain `closed_month` (the new day's month, else the old day's), and `closed_from_month` (the old day's) for a shift moved from one closed month into another; `notify_for_payroll` (P4's, copied) tells each such month's closer and the accountants `pay.after_close` (dedupe `pay.after_close:<month>`, back unread with each later change) and settles `pay.month_due` on `payroll.close`, its trigger now `permission.grant`, `permission.revoke`, `wage.set`, `payroll.close` and only the clock rows that carry `closed_month` or `closed_from_month`; `private.payroll_tick(at_time)` and the pg_cron job `payroll` (`0 6 * * *` UTC; `seed.sql` switches it off with the notifications' two): on a business's own 2nd, with the time clock on, not a demo, and last month holding hours and not closed, its pay holders get `pay.month_due` with how many shifts wait for a confirmation and are open (dedupe `payroll.month:<month>`, never told twice); `purge_business_records` (P5's) deletes the months before the log. pgTAP: `payroll_months.test.sql` new (60: grants and the job; the reminder on the 3rd and the 2nd with its counts, once, none in a demo, a manager given pay access told as a pay holder; an open shift counted, a business with no hours told nothing, a month that ends by the business's clock and not UTC's (23:30 and 00:30 in Israel), and a month closed before its tick never told; a manager, a manager given pay access, a worker and the team refused, the current month and no snapshot refused, the accountant closing, the row and its log, the reminder settled, closing twice; a wage into the month refused and one from the next month saved; a correction and an added shift going through with `closed_month`, one line for the accountant who closed it, none for the owner or the manager, a November correction naming none, a shift moved out of the closed month naming it, and one moved from one closed month into another naming both, with a line to each month's closer and the accountant; reopening without a reason or with a blank one, by a manager or one given pay access, then by the owner with the row and the log keeping the reason, twice refused, a wage then saved and a correction naming no month, closed again anew; who reads the months; nothing deletes one but the purge); `rls_enabled.test.sql` names the table. `database.types.ts` gains `payroll_months`, `close_payroll_month` and `reopen_payroll_month`, by hand in the generator's format. Domain: `@domain/payroll-diff` (`closedSnapshot`: the engine's month without amounts, the pay rules alone, the business's place (its city, a missing one as Tel Aviv, and time zone) and the people as the engine read them; `readSnapshot`, by Zod, null for anything else; `closedPeople`; `closedReads`, what a closed month is worked out again with (its own place, a snapshot without one taking the place now); `monthDifferences(closed, now)`, per person, per day and per rate, minutes only, with the shifts behind each day; `changedDays`), `@domain/payroll-close` (`closesMonths`, `monthEnded`, `closeFailureOf`), `@domain/wages` (`parseWageForm` refuses a day of a closed month, `firstOpenDay`, `monthClosed` for 55000), `@domain/payroll-export` (a fifth sheet "הפרשים" before "על הקובץ", `DIFF_COLUMNS`: name, number, the month it belongs to, date, weekday, hours then and now, the difference in all and at each rate, why; still no amount; the sheet about the file says when the month before closed, and whether it or the engine changed since), `@domain/notifications` (`pay.after_close`; `pay.month_due`, which needs action; both lead to that month's `/clock/pay?month=`), `@domain/activity` (`payroll.close`, `payroll.reopen` with its reason), each with tests (`payroll-diff.test.ts` new, with plan §12.2's 30 minutes more on a 9-hour day as `ot125 +30`, and the path the screen takes, through JSON and `readSnapshot`: no difference after a new wage and a move to Jerusalem, a correction the only one, and Friday's minutes moving between rates if the city now leaked in; `payroll-close.test.ts` new). DB layer: `@db/payroll-months` (`getPayrollMonthRecord` and `getClosedMonths` under the person's session, nothing before the migration; `closePayrollMonth` and `reopenPayrollMonth` with the secret key). App: `currentPayrollReads` gives the people the engine read; `currentClosedMonth(month)` works a closed month out again from its shifts now, with the settings, place and people it closed with (`closedReads`), against its snapshot; `closeMonthAction` and `reopenMonthAction` (bound on the page to the business the screen was drawn for, "businessChanged" after a switch; never in a view; an owner or an accountant; the reason read by `parseReason`); `/clock/pay` keeps "הורדת הקובץ לשכר" as its main action and gets `MonthCloseAction` beside it (one component for both states, so what a press says stays while the page reads the month again): "סגירת החודש" for a month that has ended, with "אחרי הסגירה תיקונים יופיעו כהפרשים בחודש הבא." and no confirmation, then "נסגר ב-3.11 ע״י גלית הדגמה" under the title and "פתיחת החודש מחדש", a sheet that asks why; a closed month says how many days changed since and which month's file takes them, and the month after says its file holds them; a manager with pay access reads it closed and presses nothing; the wage sheet starts after the closed months, says so, and refuses a day in one in words, as the action does. The inbox and the phone word both lines; the activity log reads both actions. Messages `app.pay.close.*`, `app.pay.reopen.*`, `app.pay.diff.*`, `app.pay.file.sheets.differences`, `columns.differences`, `differenceRate` and `about.closed*`, the closed month's in `app.pay.wage`, `app.notifications.kinds.pay.after_close` and `.month_due`, `app.manage.activity.actions.payroll.close`, `.reopen` and `.reopenReason`, he and en; `tooling/payroll-words.test.ts` holds the sheet's columns and sentences, and `file.test.ts` runs them in the real words. `/design/shell`: `clock/pay?close=closed|changed|manager|before` and `clock/pay/noa?close=closed` (demo stand-ins that store nothing), in `design.spec.ts`'s sweep at every size with axe, and a test that closes, reads the header, reopens through the sheet with a reason (refused without), reads the changed and the next month's lines, and has a wage refused in October; the activity demo has October closed, reopened and closed again. e2e: `payroll-close.spec.ts` new (at every size Galit closes the seeded month beside its file, the header says so, axe, and she reopens it through the sheet with a reason; on the desktop, closed again, the shop's manager corrects one of Roni's shifts of it through `correct_time_entry`, its row names the month, Galit gets one `pay.after_close` line and reads it in her inbox, the month says its differences go into the next month's file, and she reopens it, the log keeping the reason; the demo shop is reset after the spec). Deviations from the plan: the migration is `…001000`; the kinds are `pay.after_close` and `pay.month_due` (§12.1 says `payroll.month_due`, but §12.2's words are `kinds.pay.month_due`, and a line's words follow its kind, as `pay.wage_set`'s), with §12.1's dedupe keys; the trigger keeps `permission.revoke` (§12.1's list leaves it out, which would stop settling access taken back); the activity words are `actions.payroll.close` and `reopen` beside P5's and P8's `actions.payroll.*`, not `payroll_close`; demo businesses get no reminder at the reset (§12.1 says they get it in the app there): the task's rule is never for a demo business, P5 left `seed_demo_pay` alone for P9, and a line that needs action after every reset would put a count on every demo pay holder's Home that no spec expects; the reminder skips a month with no hours; the snapshot keeps no amount (they follow from the minutes and wages it keeps), and a closed month is worked out again with its own settings and people, so a later change of settings or details makes no difference, while a new engine version may, and the file says so; "הפרשים" holds the month just before the file's, not every closed month before it, so no difference is in two files; the e2e makes its correction through the database function rather than `/clock/team`. Checked: Biome, `next typegen`, tsc, and vitest over packages, modules, app and tooling (the Windows-only Biome probe aside); by reading, the migration's and the pgTAP's dollar quotes, `plan(60)` against its assertions, every copied function against its newest definition (`set_hourly_wage` P5's, `add_time_entry` and `correct_time_entry` P1's, `notify_for_payroll` and its trigger P4's, `purge_business_records` P5's; `private.notify`'s and `private.resolve_notifications`' arguments), the typed literals, and that no parameter is named like a column it reads. Not run here (no database, no CI): pgTAP, `supabase db lint`, `pnpm db:types`, the build, the e2e and the screens at 390, 820 and 1440 px. After review: the after-close line names the month whose file takes the differences (`kinds.pay.after_close.note` `{next}`, `noteUndated` without a month) in the inbox and on the phone; a closed month whose next month is closed too says to download that file again (`diff.nextClosed`, `MonthClosing.nextClosed`); a month with no hours but the month before's differences keeps its file as the main action and says it holds them; the wage sheet's hint names the closed month and the real first day (`wage.sheet.fromHintClosed`, replacing `fromClosed`); the `/design/shell` file follows the screen (`demoPayPathsFor`, `?close=before` and the new `?close=empty` twin with `demoPayDifferences`), checked in `design.spec.ts` by the workbook's sheets; `payroll-close.spec.ts` downloads the next month's file and finds Roni's corrected day with its month and reason on "הפרשים", reads the inbox line's month, and the next month's screen. Next: an accountant's and a lawyer's look at the engine (plan §14) before a real business turns the estimate on.
- Lists L2 fixes from a look at the live demo, on chat/lists-live-fixes over `9f9c8c1` (#91): a range or reading below zero is written with a real minus sign, and the dash is spaced once one is in it ("−25 – −18 °C", which read as "-25–-18"); a main `DockButton` never fades, since the list under the dock showed through a disabled "סיום הרשימה": it turns to glass while it can't be pressed and keeps its colour while it works. - Lists L3, templates and the full send, on chat/lists-templates over `3cdf57d` (P3b, #104), following the lists plan's §1.3, §5, §8 to §11 and §13 with its critique applied, Orel's defaults on #1, and L1, L2 and its fixes (#90 to #92) as built. No migration: L1's `save_list_template`, `archive/restore_list_template` and `send_list` (with L2's `cancel_unstarted_list` for the undo) do it all, so `database.types.ts` is unchanged. `/lists/templates` (list managers; a view session as a manager only reads): the business's templates by the department each goes to, then the rest (to a role, a shift, or chosen each time), an order flagged as one; one group under the other on a phone, two columns on a tablet, one table with who asks for each order on a computer; the main action "יצירת רשימה"; removed ones folded at the end, each with "החזרה", and the one just removed in its editor with its undo on top (`?removed=`); an empty business says what to make. `/lists/templates/[id]` and `/new`: the editor saves itself on every change (a tap at once, typing half a second after it stops or on leaving the field), one save at a time and the newest last, each with the version it last had, so a save someone else's overtook is refused (40001) and the editor opens again with the latest and says so (`?changed=`, keyed by the version); a new template saves once it has a name and an item and takes its own address without drawing the screen again (`history.replaceState`); name and note; items of check, number (unit, range, a "מינוס" toggle for a bound below zero) and note, required or not, up and down on each row, a sheet to add, change or remove one, and the undo of a removal (an item removed and saved comes back as a new row, `itemsForSave`); who it goes to (each time, a department, a shift of the scheduling settings or a role; an order only to another department); "הסרת הרשימה" with its undo on the screen of templates; the main action "שליחת הרשימה עכשיו" saves what's pending, then opens the send screen with it. A recipient that is gone (a removed department, a shift no longer in the settings) opens unchosen and says so, since the database takes it in no save. What the editor doesn't edit yet goes back as it was read: the times (L4) and an order's department, quantities and usual counts (L5) (`templateInputOf`). The console: `/admin/businesses/[slug]/lists`, `/new` and `/[id]` draw the same screens with the team's own actions (`saveTeamListTemplateAction`, `archive/restoreTeamListTemplateAction`, a template only of that business), a "רשימות" row on its settings, and nothing to send. `/lists/send` in full: any template, or "רשימה חדשה" typed now with the same items editor; a person, a department, a published shift from today to a week ahead that hasn't ended ("ערב · שלישי 17.11 · 16:00–23:30 · 4 אנשים", one with nobody on it shown and not chosen; a template that goes to a shift starts at its next one with people), or a role; by when, "בלי שעה", today or tomorrow at a time an hour ahead to start with, refused in words once it has gone by; a note; the button names where it goes ("שליחה למשמרת ערב"); the preview from a tablet up, and on a computer what the person sent today. `/lists` gains "הרשימות של העסק" beside the business's day for list managers, and the main action for anyone who may send, templates or not. Writes bound to the business the screen was drawn for (P3b: `saveListTemplateAction`, `archive/restoreListTemplateAction`, `sendListAction` take it first and refuse another with `businessChanged`, in words). In code: `@domain/lists` gains `numberRange` and `signed` (moved from the list screen), `DraftItem`, `draftItemsOf`, `itemsForSave`, `typedItems`, `templateInputOf`, `templateDraftProblem`, `moved`, `recipientGone`, `templatesByDepartment`, `dueAtOf`, `suggestedDue`, `upcomingShifts`, `SavedTemplate`, and after review `boundOf`, `typedBound` and `readBound` (moved from the item's sheet), `templateStuck`, `sendDefaults` (moved from the send screen) and `dueTimeFor`, with tests; `@db/lists` `saveListTemplate` reads the saved items' ids after the save (the function returns only the id and the version), and once the function has saved, a failure after it carries the template's id (`saved` on `ListWrite`'s failure), `getListTemplate(business, id)`, `getListTemplatesForTeam`, `getListTemplateForTeam`, `getSentLists`; `@db/schedule` `getPublishedShifts`. App-level, not `@ui` (so nothing new for `/design` or CLAUDE.md's list): `ItemsEditor`, `TemplateEditor`, `TemplatesView`, `ListPreview` and `SentToday`. Messages `app.lists.templates.*`, `app.lists.items.*`, `app.lists.templatesRow`, the rest of `app.lists.send` and `admin.settings.lists`, he and en. `/design/shell/lists/templates` (with `?removed=`, `?state=empty` and `?as=viewing`, a view session), `/templates/[id]` (with `?changed=1`, `?as=viewing`, `?state=barGone` for an order whose department was removed, and the removed `t-stock-count`, only read), `/templates/new`, `/send?template=new` and `?state=kitchenGone`, and the console's `/design/console/businesses/[slug]/lists` and `/[id]`, from mock templates in `app/design/shell/lists/data.ts` (already listed in `tooling/hebrew-literals.ts`), all in `design.spec.ts` with a test of the editor's sheets (axe on the new item's and on one already there), the stuck order, the console's twin, a template whose department was removed on the send screen and a typed list sent to a shift. e2e: `list-templates.spec.ts` new (finding and reading the templates and the new item's sheet at every width; on the desktop only, as each writes the shared demo: a template of every kind made, a bound below zero by the toggle and one typed with its minus, saved by itself, reordered by pointer and by keyboard (the focus kept, the move said), an item removed and put back by its undo, renamed, overtaken by a save elsewhere and opened again with the latest (said once, `?changed=` gone from the address), its recipient saved as a department, a role and each time, removed and brought back by the undo and again from the folded list (axe on it), then sent to Noa from its editor; a list typed now sent to next Sunday's evening shift, due tomorrow; the team making one in the console; each removing what it made); `lists.spec.ts`'s view session also reads `/lists/templates` and an editor that changes nothing. After review: a minus typed in a bound is kept and turns the toggle on; a new template whose save may have gone through without its answer stops saving and says so with the way to the business's lists (`templates.failed.createUnknown`), and one whose id came back opens at its own address, so none is made twice; up and down keep the focus on the item's arrows and say where it went (`items.movedTo`, a polite live region); "changed on another screen" goes once a save passes and leaves the address; a template whose department was removed starts the send screen with nobody chosen and says so (`send.gone`), and an order whose department was removed can't be sent by its department (`send.orderGone`, the main action off); choosing today late in the evening starts at a time still to come (`dueTimeFor`, until the person sets one). Deviations from the plan: the editor's desktop is its tablet's two columns (the last sending and the readings come with L4); removing is from the editor, with the undo and a folded "removed" section on the screen of templates; the item ids come from a read after the save rather than from a changed function, which keeps L3 without a migration, and a create lost after the function saved is stopped and said rather than made safe to retry (an id made by the screen would need a migration); a template the database would refuse whatever the editor does (`templateStuck`: an order whose asking department was removed, until L5 lets an order's department be changed, or a time that follows a shift, or goes to a department or a shift, that is gone, until L4 lets the times be edited) opens only to read, says why, and can still be removed or sent; a time sent to someone who has left still reads as "invalid", since the editor doesn't read the people. Checked: Biome, `next typegen`, tsc and vitest over packages, modules, app and tooling (the Windows-only Biome probe aside). Not run here: the build, the e2e (`list-templates.spec.ts` new, `design.spec.ts`, `lists.spec.ts` and `e2e/db.ts` changed), pgTAP (nothing new), and the screens at 390, 820 and 1440 px. Next: L4, the times and the manager's overview.
- Lists L4, times, due and late, on chat/lists-schedules over chat/lists-templates (L3, #106, which has no migration of its own), following the lists plan's §1.4, §5, §6, §7, §10.4, §10.5, §11, §13 and §14 with its critique applied and Orel's defaults on #1 (a missed daily list is "לא נעשתה" when the next opens or 24 hours after its due time; a business closed on Shabbat gets no list after candle lighting on Friday, and Saturday's at havdalah). `20261110001300_list_schedules.sql` (after P9's `001000`; the plan's `20261112000100` was a placeholder): `private.shabbat_times` (candle lighting and havdalah for the eight cities, every Friday from 6 November 2026 to the end of 2031, 2,152 rows printed by `tooling/shabbat-table.test.ts` with `SHABBAT_TABLE=print`, which checks every row against `@domain/shabbat-times` and fails a year before they run out), `private.closes_on_shabbat` and `private.shabbat_span` (Tel Aviv for no city or one no longer listed), `private.list_occurrences` (the business's own clock, a skipped spring time an hour later with its length kept when that would pass its due time, the later of an autumn hour, minutes around a published shift of its pattern and past midnight, one a day, nothing before the time was saved or on a day before it starts, nothing for a pattern the settings lost, Shabbat's three rules), `private.lists_tick(at_time, for_business)` over `private.lists_tick_business` (each business in its own block; opens once by the schedule's day with the 12-hour catch-up, to the time's recipient or the role manager when nobody is there: a person who left, an empty or unpublished shift, a removed department; the 500 open lists cap under the business's lock; missed, then due, then late, each `for update skip locked`; nothing but missed during a closed business's Shabbat), the job `lists` every minute (off in `seed.sql` with the others), `public.next_list_openings` (three a time from the same function, list managers and the team), `set_list_item` (L1's) logging `lists.out_of_range` when a reading moves outside its range, `private.notify_for_lists` (L1's) with `lists.due` (pushed until due), `lists.late`, `lists.late_team` (the managers who don't hold it, while `tellManagersWhenLate`) and `lists.out_of_range`, and `private.seed_demo_lists` (L2's) ageing the demo's times two days and ticking where the definition says `"scheduledLists": true` (the hosted two, patched here and in `hosted-demo.sql`; `tooling/demo-profiles.test.ts` keeps the local seed's without it), so no reset in a test, and no browser test, depends on the hour; its examples are of templates nothing opened today. Both hosted demo businesses reset. pgTAP `lists_schedule.test.sql` (74). `database.types.ts` gains `next_list_openings`, by hand in the generator's format. In TS: `@domain/list-openings` (the same rules, pure, for a time the editor hasn't saved: `listOpenings`, `nextOpenings`, `dueOpenings`, `wallInstant`, `shabbatTouches`, with tests across a week, both clock changes, shifts past midnight, Friday after candle lighting and Saturday at havdalah), `shabbatSpansFrom` in `@domain/shabbat-times`, and in `@domain/lists` `DraftSchedule`, `draftSchedulesOf`, `schedulesForSave`, `scheduleGone`, `scheduleProblem`, `dayRuns`, `ListSchedule.createdAt`, `SavedTemplate.schedules` and `.openings`, `templateInputOf` taking the editor's times, and `templateStuck` now only for an order (a time pointing at something gone is changed or removed in the editor, which holds its saves until then); the four kinds with their words, inbox and push cases (`late_team` and `out_of_range` ask managers to act); activity cases for `lists.late`, `lists.missed` and `lists.out_of_range`; `@db/lists` `nextListOpenings`, `getListReadings`, `getBusinessPlaceForTeam`, and `saveListTemplate` reading back the times' ids and openings. `DayToggles` in `@ui` (on /design, and the shifts editor uses it). The editor's "מתי היא נשלחת": a row a time (its days in runs, its hour or its place around a shift, by when, to whom, and when it is sent next, from the database once saved and from the rules while not), a sheet to add, change, pause or remove one with its undo, the Shabbat line where it applies; the console's editor too, with the business's place and people read with the secret key. `/lists/today` (list managers; a view session as a manager only reads): late first, open, closed and missed, the day's readings out of range first and the week's, a week strip for the last seven days (`?day=`), cards on a phone, two columns on a tablet, the day's table and the week's readings on a computer; "היום בעסק" on Lists and Today leads there. `/design/shell/lists/today` (with `?day=2026-11-01`, `?state=empty`, `?state=viewing`), the demo templates' times and last night's late closing with a fridge out of range, in `design.spec.ts` with a test of the times sheet and the history. e2e `lists-schedule.spec.ts`: the day and the sheet clean at every width; on a computer, a template of its own, a time saved through the sheet saying when it is sent next, `tickLists` opening it late once, Noa seeing and hearing it and reading the fridge out of range, the manager's day with it first and the week's reading, and the day before. Deviations from the plan: a TS engine beside the SQL one (the task asked for it; the sheet shows a time not yet saved, rows show the database's); "כמה זמן יש" and minutes before a shift are menus rather than a Stepper; scheduled lists that fall back to the managers aren't marked "אין אף אחד במשמרת" on the overview; the editor's desktop has no "last sent" or readings column (the overview has the readings); demo schedules aren't paused in e2e but local resets open none (an opt-in flag on the hosted definitions); CLAUDE.md's "Built so far" still needs `DayToggles` added (left to Orel). Checked: Biome, `next typegen`, tsc, vitest over packages, modules, app and tooling (the Windows-only Biome probe aside), and the SQL by reading (dollar quotes, plan(74) against 74 assertions, argument counts against the newest definitions). ## 2026-11-09 · chat · QR clock-in, part A: the code on a manager's phone, typed digits, without a code

- On chat/qr-v1, after `qr` 0.7.2 (`825b6ae`), following the plan for #78 with its critique applied. No new migration: it runs on `20261108000200_qr_clock.sql` and `20261108000300_sealed.sql`, and merges only once the hosted project has the sealed one (the four-argument `note_punch_attempt`, owner-only code settings).
- The owner asks for it: `requirePunchCode` is drawn now, at a new setting level `owner` (owners and the team; managers don't see it, and the database refuses it from them), with a "לבעלים בלבד" badge. `codeDisplay` stays hidden until stations exist, so codes come only from an owner's or manager's phone.
- `/clock/code`, from the header of Team hours and a row on the clock: a QR (`ca1:` and 16 random bytes, not a link) and six digits under it, a new code every 20 s, valid 60 s, from the route `POST /clock/code/issue` (same origin only, `no-store`), timed on the database's clock and the phone's monotonic one. A code with 20 s or more left stays through failed fetches; the screen keeps itself awake where the phone allows, and offers full screen where it can. The digits are an HMAC with a key derived from `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` (`packages/db/punch-key.ts`): nothing new to set in Vercel.
- The worker: with a code asked for, the circle opens a sheet for the digits; breaks need none. Five wrong tries in 30 minutes block typing (each counted first, as the database wants); "without a code" is always one press away, keeps the real time, and marks the entry. A plain press after the owner switched codes on says to press again, not that digits were wrong.
- Approving: a card on Team hours lists what this person may approve (a manager not their own; a manager's own, an owner only), with Approve and a link to fix; Today shows the count and leads there. No undo for an approval: fixing the shift is enough (Orel's call). Entries made without a code count in the hours, with a mark: each shift says how it was recorded, and "מחכה לאישור" while it waits. The log reads approvals and typing blocks.
- New in @ui: `QrCode`, `CodeDisplay`, `useWakeLock`, CodeSlots' `autoComplete`, and `qr-ink`/`qr-paper` tokens (dark on white in every appearance), with a gallery section. The worker's clock still downloads no Zod (`@domain/punch-code-format`, guarded by a boundaries test).
- Checked locally: Biome, tsc and every unit test but the Windows-only Biome probe. Not run here: the build, the e2e (a new `clock-code.spec.ts`, phone only, and changes to the clock, team, manage and design specs), and the screens at 390, 820 and 1440 px.
- Next: build and e2e on CI, screenshots, the PR; then part B, scanning inside the circle.
- Profile H3 (#82), on chat/profile-validators: the checks the hiring file will use, pure and tested in `@domain`, no screen and no migration yet. `typed-text` (one cleaner for direction marks, Arabic and Persian digits, dashes and odd spaces), `israeli-id`, `identity-documents` (passport, another document, ISO countries plus XK), `banks` (Masav's participants as of 16.09.2026, names under `banks` in the messages), `bank-account` (Masav's dated rules: valid, a warning the person may save past, or unchecked; the paste splitter and the Israeli IBAN) and `address` (zip, house number, street, area or P.O. box). Tested on Masav's own examples where it gives one; the rest (HSBC, Leumi's other types, the branch exceptions, Beinleumi's step (b)) are built by the rule. Guessed, for Orel: lengths count digits without leading zeros, and groups 31, 26 and 52 take up to nine digits where Masav writes 2-6 (its two steps only differ past six); 09 is Masav's new name "דואר פיננסים", not "בנק הדואר"; 18 is "וואן זירו" (Masav: "בנק וואן זירו"); no exception for Leumi branch 864, which the rule doesn't give; `COUNTRIES_AS_OF` is written from memory, not checked against ISO. A paste with labels but no branch or no account is read by position only when that agrees with the labels. Checked: Biome, tsc and the new tests. Next: H1.
- Part B, on chat/qr-scan over part A (#83): with a code asked for, pressing the circle turns it into the camera, in the same place (`ScanCircle` in @ui: the business's colour as a ring around a round picture, a frame to aim with, one polite status line). The back camera at 1280×720, the middle square of a frame read about 8 times a second at up to 480 px: by the phone's own BarcodeDetector where it reads QR codes (Android), by the `qr` decoder otherwise (iPhones, most computers; `@domain/qr-decode`, loaded only when the clock asks for a code, and not at all on a phone with its own reader). A `ca1:` code clocks in or out at once with its hash; the picture freezes, the phone buzzes, a check and the time show, then the live circle. A code refused for itself isn't sent again, so a camera still on it doesn't hammer the server; one that isn't the clock's says so. The camera is off unless that circle shows: typing, "without a code", stopping, the page hidden or left, and two minutes with nothing read all turn it off. Asking, refused and missing cameras each have their line, and typing becomes the main action. Typing and "without a code" stay under the circle; "back" from without a code goes to scanning, which a typing block doesn't stop. An iPhone home-screen app asks about the camera each time it starts and loses it in the background: the screen says so and opens it again on return.
- Words switched to scanning (the setting's hint, the code screen's "how" and digits label, the circle, the typing block). New e2e: a worker scans the manager's code in and out (a code the business never showed is refused first), a manager scanning their own code is refused, and a refused or missing camera leads to the digits; CI has no camera, so the worker's page gets a canvas's `captureStream` as its camera, painted from the code screen's own path, and no BarcodeDetector, so the decoder iPhones use is the one tested. Unit tests draw a code with the encoder and read it back.
- Checked locally: Biome, tsc and the affected unit tests. Not run here: the build, the e2e, the screens at 390, 820 and 1440 px, and real phones (plan 12.5: an Android installed app, an iPhone home-screen app, Safari), which CI can't do and which go in the PR before it is done. The future `Permissions-Policy` must allow `camera=(self)` (and `screen-wake-lock=(self)`, `fullscreen=(self)` for part A).

## 2026-11-08 · chat · the security check, sealing the database, the door on tablets

- Orel asked for a full security check "from all directions and all roles". Eight directions and four follow-ups, every finding re-checked by a skeptic: no critical or high finding, and tenant isolation, server-side authorization, secrets, XSS, CSRF, redirects and the invite and punch tokens held. The fix list is #80, with Orel's own steps (Supabase test numbers, Turnstile, the Vercel secret for Production only, a GitHub environment and ruleset, Dependabot) as a comment there. He chose: security before QR; keep the "number isn't registered" hint and add limits; a manager's own hours allowed but marked for the owner; the console makes an owner only of a business without one. On #1.
- The database half, on chat/sealed (`20261108000300_sealed.sql`): demo businesses take no invites and nobody joins one (the local seed opens its two with `"invites": true` for the browser tests, and a tooling test keeps `hosted-demo.sql` closed); the console's owner rule; limits on sign-in codes per number and per caller (`private.auth_limits`), with a try given back when the auth server refused for its own wait; hours a manager or `clock.edit` holder sets for themselves marked `self` for an owner to confirm, a corrected time resting on `manager` instead of its old scan, a station one paired counting as `self`; each typed guess counted on its own; the punch code settings for owners and the team, in SQL; colleagues' numbers only through `team_phones` for managers, one's own through `own_phone`; photos written by the server only. The app runs before the migration does, so the order is merge, deploy, migrate.
- Its review (five angles, a skeptic each) confirmed eleven problems, all fixed; the main ones were an old test expecting the correction's log without its proofs, an owner's new link for a console-sent owner invite giving a dead link, and the deploy order. The limits hold the app's own way in only; the public auth API meets Supabase's and Twilio's limits and CAPTCHA, now in the README's checklist.
- Orel's phone (an opened foldable, wider than 768px) showed the entrance as the tablet card. The sign-in, entrance and join screens now keep the phone layout up to desktop, in a column of readable width; "ב-SMS" keeps together with a word joiner.
- Orel asked (8 November) for a personal profile for everyone, with the details for hiring (Form 101, ID, birth date, bank account) and a photo, a description and a banner. Research with fact-checks is running; it changes the payroll plan's "no bank details" (#76). On #1.
- Next: CI and merge this, then migrate; the app half of #80; QR v1 screens; the profile design for Orel.

## 2026-11-08 · chat · the logo, CI minutes, and the way to the stores

- Actions runs again: the free 2,000 minutes had run out (about 2,070 used from 1 to 8 November, 80% of it e2e, 32% reruns on main of trees already tested), and Orel bought GitHub Pro (3,000). The Migrate workflow applied #68's and #69's migrations (a dry run first showed exactly those two), #73 merged after its CI passed, and its push run on main was cancelled because main's tree was the one just tested.
- A study of the minutes (four angles, two skeptics) found about 35–40% to save with no test dropped: skip the push run when the same tree passed a PR run that day, a 2 s SMS wait in local auth with a test that trips it, reusing each person's session per size once they signed in through the screen, starting Supabase during the build. Even then usage stays above 3,000 a month, so Orel was told a budget in Billing (about $10–25 a month) and branch protection on main are his calls. Built on chat/ci-minutes, all but the session reuse (the riskiest, next on its own): the tree gate with a record per job, the 2 s wait with a test that restarts the wait in `auth.users` right before each refused request so it does not depend on the runner's speed, one database connection per worker for the e2e helpers, and Supabase starting while Chromium installs. Its review (three angles, a skeptic) confirmed one problem, the README still saying main is re-tested on every push, fixed.
- A study of the stores (four researchers, a fact-check): one system, the store apps open the live site. Android as a Trusted Web Activity, the iPhone as a Capacitor shell that needs native parts to pass Apple's 4.2 (a native QR scanner, haptics, notifications later). Both stores want a privacy policy, account deletion in the app and a reviewer sign-in; Apple's company account wants a D-U-N-S number and our own domain. Orel is opening a company and will pick the domain.
- Orel decided (8 November 2026) that clocking in and out is by QR. The database already has it (`issue_punch_code`, `requirePunchCode`); the screens are missing. On issue #1.
- The new logo and app icon, chosen by Orel from ChatGPT's designs: the mark redrawn as vectors from measured shapes, the wordmark traced, `@ui/logo` (`Logo`, `LogoMark`) on the sign-in screens and in /design, every icon size from the same shapes, the brand files and their README in `docs/brand`. A review (three angles, a skeptic) confirmed three problems, all fixed: the store icons had an alpha channel, which App Store Connect refuses; `brand-2` was mixed in oklab, paler than the files; Google Play wants a 512 icon.
- Orel asked (8 November) for an accounting role in every business, also a permission for a role or a member, that sees everyone's hours and exports them for payroll under Israeli law, and an hourly wage per worker that owners, managers and accounting change. He chose: an accountant inside one business and an external one across several, from the start; a worker sees their own wage; after QR clock-in. On issue #1. Research on the law, payroll formats and the repo is running.
- Next: the minutes PR, then QR clock-in and what both stores require; the store apps once Orel has the company's accounts and domain.

- The rest of wording, on this branch: `@domain/wording` (which sentences, what a replacement must keep, layers, the merge), the member's business carrying its sentences at no extra round, a translator over the business's words passed down as a value, `WordingProvider` sending the browser only the replaced sentences, every server view moved over (four agents, one area each, in separate worktrees), and the console editor at /admin/businesses/[slug]/wording with search, a sheet beside the app's and the template's sentence, a server check that the sentence keeps its parts and formats, and undo. Orel was asked to check Billing and plans.

## 2026-11-06 · run 29 · profile photos, database and server (#42)

- Left by Orel: #42, the second item of the run. No screens; `packages/ui` (including the new `Avatar`) untouched.
- Migration `profile_photos`: a private Storage bucket `avatars` (JPEG, PNG or WebP, up to 2MB, enforced by the bucket too), `profiles.avatar_path` with a check that it points into the person's own folder (`<user id>/<key>.<ext>`), and policies on `storage.objects`: only the person uploads, replaces and removes in their own folder; the person and active members of a business they are active in read. Other businesses, former colleagues and anonymous visitors read nothing; the public URL does not work.
- `@domain/profile-photos`: the type is read from the file's first bytes and must match what the browser says; size, paths. `@db/profile-photos`: `setProfilePhoto` (check, upload, point the profile, remove the old photo), `removeProfilePhoto`, `profilePhotoUrls` (signed links for an hour, signed with the person's own session, so Storage refuses paths they may not read).
- Proof: pgTAP `profile_photos.test.sql` (20), 8 unit tests (349 in all), `pnpm check`, `pnpm build`, db lint. Storage itself is not in CI's database job, so I also ran the full local stack and checked the real Storage API with a script: GIF and 2MB+1 refused, upload into a colleague's folder refused, a colleague gets a signed link and a stranger or anonymous visitor does not, the signed link downloads (200), the public URL does not (400).
- Defaults: "view as" does not see photos (the issue says active colleagues only), so it shows initials. `avatar_path` is written only by the server. A new photo gets a new random path, so old links stop working.
- Next: the screens by day (a photo picker in the profile, `Avatar` showing the photo).

## 2026-11-06 · run 29 · time clock, database and logic (#41)

- Left by Orel: #41 and #42 (label `orel`, from chat), fired in the afternoon rather than at 02:00. This entry is #41; #42 (profile pictures) follows in its own pull request. No screens; tokens and `packages/ui` untouched.
- Restore point: the tag `before-night/2026-11-06-run29` was refused (403), so the branch `backup/before-night/2026-11-06-run29` at `6a3f9c2` holds it. The day already had a restore point from run 28, hence the suffix. #40 (design foundation, a chat session) merged after it; an undo keeps #40.
- Migration `time_clock`: table `time_entries` (clock in, clock out or open, `punch` or `manager`, note, who edited). An exclusion constraint keeps one person's entries in a business from overlapping, so there is at most one open entry; an entry is at most 24 hours. Table `punch_codes` holds only SHA-256 hashes, for two minutes, with no policies at all. Server-only functions `issue_punch_code`, `clock_in`, `clock_out`, `add_time_entry`, `correct_time_entry`, each written to `audit_log` (a correction keeps the old and new times). RLS: your own entries; owners, managers, holders of `clock.edit` and "view as" at manager the business's; nothing with the module off.
- `@domain/time-clock`: minutes in an entry, the week's summary per day and in all, totals per person, who is at work now, rounding, and the refusals mapped for the screens. `@db/time-clock`: making and hashing codes, the five writes and `getTimeEntries`. Manifest setting `requirePunchCode`.
- Proof: pgTAP `time_clock.test.sql` (48), `rls_enabled` lists the two tables, 20 unit tests (296 in all), `pnpm check`, `pnpm build`, `supabase db lint`, `pnpm test:db`, all against a local Supabase this time.
- Defaults: a code is required to clock in and out (setting `requirePunchCode`, on for every business); managers' additions and corrections need none. Codes are issued by owners, managers and `clock.edit`, last two minutes, and the device shows a fresh one every 30 seconds. An entry counts on the day it started, so an evening past midnight stays on the evening. An inactive worker cannot clock out; a manager closes the entry. There is no delete of an entry yet.
- Merged: `79abd7d` (PR #43).
- Next: #42, then the clock screens by day, in the new design.

## 2026-11-05 · run 26 · chunk 2d-1 (shift swaps in the database)

- No open questions or instructions; `main` green at `2505d0a`. Split 2d
  (shift swaps) in two: 2d-1 the database (today), 2d-2 the screens.
- Migration `shift_swaps`: table `shift_swaps` (open → taken → approved /
  declined, or cancelled by the worker) and server-only functions
  `request_shift_swap`, `take_shift_swap`, `cancel_shift_swap`,
  `decide_shift_swap`, each written to `audit_log`. Rules: only your own
  shift of a published week, before it starts (business time zone); one
  live request per shift and person; the colleague is an active member,
  not on the shift and on no other shift at the same time; an active
  manager or owner decides, and on approval every rule is checked again
  and the colleague takes the worker's place in the same position.
- RLS: active members of the business (and "view as") read its swaps;
  other businesses, inactive members and anonymous read nothing; module
  off closes it.
- Tests: pgTAP `shift_swaps.test.sql` (36). Supabase images could not be
  pulled in this run's container, so I ran the migrations and the test on
  a plain Postgres 16 with stand-ins for `auth` and pgTAP: 36/36, and four
  planted bugs (no overlap check, no "already started", wrong person
  moved, open read policy) each turned tests red. `pnpm check`, `pnpm
  build`. First CI run failed: the RLS meta-test keeps a list of tables
  and I had not added `shift_swaps` to it; added. `database.types.ts` edited by hand; CI checks it.
- Commit: squash of PR `agent/2d-1-swaps-db` (see the daily note in #1).
- Next: chunk 2d-2, the screens: "give away" on my shifts, open requests
  for colleagues to take, and the manager's approve/decline list.
- Defaults applied (no question opened): a manager always approves (no
  direct swaps); the colleague does not need to have marked availability;
  publishing changes to a week drops its pending requests (its shifts are
  replaced); owners and managers can also give away and take shifts.
